# MEDULLA-UNIFIED-DATA-PLANE requirements

| ID | Requirement | Verification |
|---|---|---|
| `MEDULLA-UNIFIED-DATA-PLANE-R001` | **Record attached-source and native-hosting decisions.** Subsystem: sources. The record is `docs/architecture/unified_data_plane_adr.md`. It holds five accepted decisions. First, attached sources are the default path and Medulla reads them as virtual graphs. Second, Medulla stays the one converged query engine. Third, Medulla keeps no synced second store. A copy is allowed only as the watermark-bound cache of MEDULLA-UNIFIED-DATA-PLANE-R038. Fourth, native hosting is earned per application through the gate of MEDULLA-UNIFIED-DATA-PLANE-R026. Fifth, the `pgrx` companion extension is a no-go. Mirror sinks and a shared relational platform are out of scope. The document is the only artifact; no runtime caller exists. Depends on: MEDULLA-UNIFIED-DATA-PLANE-R025 (retired), MEDULLA-UNIFIED-DATA-PLANE-R002.3 (retired). Caller chain: none at runtime (document-only row). The proof reads the record from the repository root: `tests/test_adr_unified_data_plane.py::test_adr_records_five_accepted_decisions` opens `docs/architecture/unified_data_plane_adr.md`. Shape: one markdown file, `docs/architecture/unified_data_plane_adr.md`, with a `**Status:** Accepted` line and exactly five `## Decision N` headings (attached sources default, one converged engine with no parallel storage authority, native hosting earned through R026, no synced store, `pgrx` go/no-go); one pytest module beside it. No Rust code and no new type. | Test: `tests/test_adr_unified_data_plane.py::test_adr_records_five_accepted_decisions` (L1) asserts five `## Decision` headings, the status `Accepted`, and the no-go wording. |
| `MEDULLA-UNIFIED-DATA-PLANE-R002` | **One owner-scoped attached-source registry (rollup).** Subsystem: sources. Entry: `Method::RegisterForeignSource`. Caller: `crates/medulla-server-router/src/dispatch/request_boundary/saga.rs` → `crates/medulla-server-ops/src/api/query_txn/foreign_catalog.rs::ForeignSourceCatalog::register`. One registry serves SQL federation and OBDA. The owner-scope rule is MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R013. Credentials resolve only by secret reference. Registration verifies the outbound target first (children `.1`, `.2`). The registry holds no kernel mirror-target registrations; `.3` is retired. A source record may name `read_role`, the read-only database role Medulla uses. This replaces the shared-platform role contract of retired R023. The dialect-adapter contract (`connect`, `render`, `read_catalog`, `decode_types`, `capture`) is delivered by R003, R006 and R008. Children `.1` and `.2` deliver the destination gate. | Test: `tests/test_uql_foreign_execution.py::test_sql_and_obda_resolve_through_one_registered_source` (L4). An unverified or cross-owner endpoint fails closed with no catalog entry. |
| `MEDULLA-UNIFIED-DATA-PLANE-R002.1` | **Verify the outbound target before persisting a `ForeignSourceSpec::Sql` registration (producer path).** `ForeignSourceCatalog::register` runs the SSRF-sensitive destination gate `medulla_federation::federation_ssrf::check_sql_dsn` against a `Sql` spec's DSN. It runs before the entry is inserted. A registration naming a disallowed address never reaches the catalog. Refusal: `CONNECTION_SECRET_INLINE`. A registration spec that carries an inline secret refuses. Only a secret reference persists. Depends on none (producer; `check_sql_dsn` and the owner-scoped catalog already exist). Caller chain: Arbora `federation.sources.register` → `Method::RegisterForeignSource` → `try_handle` (`crates/medulla-h-sparql/src/foreign_source_handlers.rs:30`, arm at line 33) → `ForeignSourceCatalog::register` (`crates/medulla-server-ops/src/api/query_txn/foreign_catalog.rs:158`, reached through the `ForeignStateExt::foreign_sources` seam in `medulla-server-ops`, so the handler crate calls no other handler) → `verify_outbound_before_register` (same file) → `medulla_federation::federation_ssrf::check_sql_dsn` (`crates/medulla-federation/src/federation_ssrf.rs:382`). Shape: edit only `crates/medulla-server-ops/src/api/query_txn/foreign_catalog.rs`. `verify_outbound_before_register` (feature `federation-sql`) already calls `check_sql_dsn` before the `entries.insert` in `register`; add a `CONNECTION_SECRET_INLINE` `&str` code constant beside it and return it when a `Sql` spec's DSN userinfo carries a password (`user:password@host`), before the destination check, so only a secret reference persists. Reuse `check_sql_dsn`, `CatalogKey` and `OwnedSpec` instead of a second gate. | A unit test registers a `Sql` spec with a disallowed destination. It confirms the catalog holds no entry afterward. A spec with an allowed destination still registers. A test asserts the code `CONNECTION_SECRET_INLINE` and zero catalog writes for an inline secret. Existing tests: `crates/medulla-server-ops/src/api/query_txn/foreign_catalog.rs::a_disallowed_sql_destination_is_refused_and_never_persisted` and `crates/medulla-server-ops/src/api/query_txn/foreign_catalog.rs::an_allowed_sql_destination_still_registers`; the inline-secret case is the planned `crates/medulla-server-ops/src/api/query_txn/foreign_catalog.rs::an_inline_secret_dsn_is_refused_and_never_persisted`. |
| `MEDULLA-UNIFIED-DATA-PLANE-R002.2` | **Verify the outbound target before persisting an OBDA `external_sources` registration (consumer path).** The OBDA table-source handler (`crates/medulla-h-sparql/src/rdf/obda.rs::register_table_sources`) runs the same destination gate against each `ObdaExternalSource`'s DSN before registering it into the `ObdaSourceRegistry`, so OBDA registration fails closed exactly like the SQL federation path. Depends on `MEDULLA-UNIFIED-DATA-PLANE-R002.1` (reuses its verification call). Caller chain: `Method::SparqlVirtual` → `try_handle_obda` (`crates/medulla-h-sparql/src/rdf/dispatch.rs:201`, call at line 231) → `handle_sparql_virtual` (`crates/medulla-h-sparql/src/rdf/obda.rs:14`) → `sparql_virtual` (obda.rs:175) → `register_table_sources` (obda.rs:224, call at line 190) → `SqlObdaSource::connect` (obda.rs:304) → `medulla_federation::federation_ssrf::check_sql_dsn` (`crates/medulla-federation/src/federation_ssrf.rs:382`). Shape: `register_table_sources` loops every `ObdaExternalSource`, collects each `connect` refusal, registers only the allowed sources into the `ObdaSourceRegistry`, and returns one `obda: refused external source(s)` error after the whole batch is inspected; `connect` reuses the R002.1 gate under `federation-sql`. No new type or file. | A unit test feeds `register_table_sources` an entry whose DSN names a disallowed destination and confirms the registry holds no source for it afterward; a disallowed entry does not block registering the other, allowed entries in the same call. Existing tests: `crates/medulla-h-sparql/src/rdf/obda.rs::a_disallowed_obda_destination_is_refused_before_registration`, `crates/medulla-h-sparql/src/rdf/obda.rs::an_allowed_obda_destination_still_connects`, `crates/medulla-h-sparql/src/rdf/obda.rs::a_refused_source_does_not_skip_later_allowed_registration`. |
| `MEDULLA-UNIFIED-DATA-PLANE-R002.3` | **RETIRED: mirror sinks replicate to a second store.** Medulla keeps no per-target mirror sink registry. The mirror sinks named by MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R024.4 and R024.5 are retired. The kernel mirror-target registration gate retires with MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R100. The process-global event sink in `crates/medulla-server-cdc/src/cdc.rs` stays an event export. | Retired; see MEDULLA-UNIFIED-DATA-PLANE-R002.1. |
| `MEDULLA-UNIFIED-DATA-PLANE-R003` | **Extract typed, hashed catalog graphs per dialect.** Each dialect adapter's catalog reader extracts tables, columns, keys, foreign keys, indexes, views, enums, and JSON columns into a versioned, typed, hashed schema graph, and every catalog artifact has a named OBDA or schema_context consumer. Net-new (no catalog-graph type existed on main): split per the net-new rule. `.1` is the typed catalog-graph model and its hash. Rollup: LANDED only once every child is. | A golden test confirms repeated catalog reads against the same source produce a stable hash and ordering. |
| `MEDULLA-UNIFIED-DATA-PLANE-R003.1` | **`AttachedCatalogGraph` is a versioned, typed catalog with a reorder-stable content hash, refusing an ambiguous duplicate table name.** `crates/medulla-catalog-types/src/attached_catalog.rs` hashes a catalog's tables, columns and views after sorting them. Two reads of the same unchanged catalog in a different discovery order hash identically. The hash now covers views. It refuses a catalog naming the same schema-qualified table twice. Caller chain: no production caller yet; the model landed ahead of its readers. The planned chain is `handle_unified_query` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/sql_read.rs:309`) → the dialect catalog reader (R014.2.2 `crates/medulla-plan/src/mysql_catalog.rs::read_catalog`, then the R017.2.2 and R020.2.2 readers) → `AttachedCatalogGraph::validate` then `content_hash`, whose result feeds the OBDA mapping and `schema_context` consumers named by R003. Shape: `crates/medulla-catalog-types/src/attached_catalog.rs`: `CatalogColumn` (name, type_name, nullable), `CatalogTable` (schema, name, columns; a view is one more `CatalogTable`, so the hash covers views), `AttachedCatalogGraph { tables, version }`, `validate` (returns `DuplicateCatalogTable` on a repeated `(schema, name)`) and `content_hash` (sorts tables by `(schema, name)` and columns by name, then hashes with `DefaultHasher`). Reuse these; add no second hasher. | Verified by unit tests. The hash is stable under table, column and view reordering. It changes when a table, column or view changes. The golden hash test holds the updated value. A duplicated schema-qualified table name is refused. Existing tests: `crates/medulla-catalog-types/src/attached_catalog.rs::content_hash_is_stable_under_table_and_column_reordering`, `crates/medulla-catalog-types/src/attached_catalog.rs::content_hash_changes_with_the_catalog`, `crates/medulla-catalog-types/src/attached_catalog.rs::a_catalog_with_one_table_per_name_validates`, `crates/medulla-catalog-types/src/attached_catalog.rs::a_duplicated_schema_qualified_table_name_is_refused`; the golden value is the planned `crates/medulla-catalog-types/src/attached_catalog.rs::content_hash_matches_golden_value`. |
| `MEDULLA-UNIFIED-DATA-PLANE-R004` | **Infer schema structure deterministically from attached catalogs.** Medulla profiles attached-source columns and infers JSON shapes under a bounded, policy-gated sampling budget, discovers inclusion and functional dependencies with supporting evidence, and groups related tables using Leiden community detection with a fixed seed. Net-new (no sampling-budget type existed on main): split per the net-new rule. `.1` is the typed sampling-budget model. Rollup: LANDED only once every child is. | A deterministic test fixture produces identical dependency and grouping results across repeated runs. |
| `MEDULLA-UNIFIED-DATA-PLANE-R004.1` | **`ColumnSamplingBudget` is a bounded, policy-gated row/byte budget, refusing a zero or unbounded declaration.** `crates/medulla-types/src/schema_inference.rs` refuses a sampling budget declaring zero rows, zero bytes, or more rows than a fixed ceiling, so JSON-shape profiling can never run with an effectively unlimited budget. Caller chain: no production caller yet. The planned chain is the JSON-shape profiling job (R004 children) → `ColumnSamplingBudget::validate` before any row is sampled from a source read through `handle_unified_query` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/sql_read.rs:309`); a refusal stops the job before it reads. Shape: `crates/medulla-types/src/schema_inference.rs`: `ColumnSamplingBudget { max_rows: u32, max_bytes: u64 }`, the ceiling `MAX_ALLOWED_SAMPLE_ROWS` (10,000,000), the `InvalidSamplingBudget` enum (`ZeroRows`, `ZeroBytes`, `RowsOverCeiling`) and `validate`. Reuse the constant; keep no second ceiling. | Verified by unit tests: a budget within bounds validates, and a zero-row, zero-byte, or over-ceiling budget is each refused. Existing tests: `crates/medulla-types/src/schema_inference.rs::a_budget_within_bounds_validates`, `crates/medulla-types/src/schema_inference.rs::a_zero_row_budget_is_refused`, `crates/medulla-types/src/schema_inference.rs::a_zero_byte_budget_is_refused`, `crates/medulla-types/src/schema_inference.rs::a_budget_over_the_ceiling_is_refused`. |
| `MEDULLA-UNIFIED-DATA-PLANE-R005` | **Compile approved relational mappings to named R2RML virtual graphs.** Medulla proposes ontology mappings deterministically first, allows a model to suggest additional mappings as proposals only, and compiles approved mappings to R2RML stored as versioned, server-side named virtual graphs queryable by name from SPARQL, UQL, and REASON. Net-new (no mapping-approval type existed on main): split per the net-new rule. `.1` is the typed approval-state model. Rollup: LANDED only once every child is. | A test confirms an unapproved or model-only mapping cannot be queried, while an approved mapping answers a known query by name. |
| `MEDULLA-UNIFIED-DATA-PLANE-R005.1` | **`VirtualGraphMapping` carries a typed approval state, refusing a query against a proposed or model-suggested mapping.** `crates/medulla-plan/src/virtual_graph_mapping.rs` returns a mapping's queryable name only when its state is `Approved`. A `Proposed` or `ModelSuggested` mapping's query is refused with a typed error naming its actual state. Caller chain: no production caller yet. The planned chain is the OBDA query path `Method::SparqlVirtual` → `handle_sparql_virtual` (`crates/medulla-h-sparql/src/rdf/obda.rs:14`) → `VirtualGraphMapping::queryable_name` before the mapping text is parsed; a `Proposed` or `ModelSuggested` state returns `MappingNotApproved`. Shape: `crates/medulla-plan/src/virtual_graph_mapping.rs`: `MappingApprovalState` (`Approved`, `Proposed`, `ModelSuggested`), `VirtualGraphMapping`, `queryable_name` (line 57) and the `MappingNotApproved` error naming the state. Reuse `validate_via_operation` in the same file for value operations; add no second approval flag. | Verified by unit tests: an approved mapping's name is queryable, and a proposed or model-suggested mapping is each refused. Existing tests: `crates/medulla-plan/src/virtual_graph_mapping.rs::an_approved_mapping_is_queryable_by_name`, `crates/medulla-plan/src/virtual_graph_mapping.rs::a_proposed_mapping_is_refused`, `crates/medulla-plan/src/virtual_graph_mapping.rs::a_model_suggested_mapping_is_refused`. |
| `MEDULLA-UNIFIED-DATA-PLANE-R006` | **Push down virtual-graph queries to source SQL.** Medulla renders joins across triples maps on the same source, aggregates, and ORDER BY/LIMIT into native source SQL using typed values rather than text casts, and shows any in-memory fallback explicitly in the query plan. Net-new (no pushdown-decision type existed on main): split per the net-new rule. `.1` is the typed decision/fallback model. Rollup: LANDED only once every child is. | A differential test compares pushed-down query results against the native source and confirms EXPLAIN reports the fallback reason when pushdown is not possible. |
| `MEDULLA-UNIFIED-DATA-PLANE-R006.1` | **`PushdownDecision` names an explicit fallback reason, refusing a decision that claims both success and a fallback.** `crates/medulla-exec/src/pushdown_plan.rs` refuses a decision where `pushed_down` is true alongside a set `fallback_reason`. EXPLAIN can therefore never report a fallback reason for a fragment that actually pushed down. Caller chain: no production caller yet. The planned chain is `handle_unified_query` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/sql_read.rs:309`) → federation fragment planning (`crates/medulla-federation/src/federation_opt`) → `PushdownDecision::validate` per fragment → EXPLAIN (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/explain_handlers.rs`), which prints a fallback reason only when `pushed_down` is false. Shape: `crates/medulla-exec/src/pushdown_plan.rs`: `PushdownFallbackReason` (closed enum), `PushdownDecision { pushed_down, fallback_reason }`, `validate` returning `InconsistentPushdownDecision`. Reuse this enum as the one EXPLAIN vocabulary; add no string reasons. | Unit tests prove three cases. A pushed-down decision with no reason validates, and a fallback decision validates. A decision claiming both is refused. Existing tests: `crates/medulla-exec/src/pushdown_plan.rs::a_pushed_down_decision_with_no_fallback_validates`, `crates/medulla-exec/src/pushdown_plan.rs::a_fallback_decision_validates`, `crates/medulla-exec/src/pushdown_plan.rs::a_contradictory_decision_is_refused`. |
| `MEDULLA-UNIFIED-DATA-PLANE-R007` | **Federate attached tables as DataFusion table providers.** Medulla exposes attached tables as DataFusion TableProviders with dialect-aware sub-plan pushdown for projection, filter, limit, join, and aggregate operations, reusing the datafusion-table-providers and datafusion-federation crates where their license permits. Net-new (no pushdown-capability type existed on main): split per the net-new rule. `.1` is the typed capability model. Rollup: LANDED only once every child is. | An integration test compares a federated query plan and result against direct execution on the source for each supported pushdown operation. |
| `MEDULLA-UNIFIED-DATA-PLANE-R008` | **Normalize change capture into a durable, replayable framework.** Each adapter emits `ChangeEnvelope` records into `CdcHub`. The `CdcHub` position store is the accepted-checkpoint store that stream pages use. `CdcHub` persists a durable position per subscription and replays idempotently after restart. It reports lag metrics with guardrails such as a WAL retention cap. It handles keyless tables explicitly. Catalog-drift detection re-runs schema intelligence and flags mappings that the drift breaks. No typed `ChangeEnvelope` model existed on main, so `.1` is the typed model. Known blocker: depends on `MEDULLA-UNIFIED-DATA-PLANE-R002`, itself `BUILDING`. Rollup: LANDED only once every child is. | A fault-injection test stops and restarts capture mid-stream. It confirms the replayed result matches a snapshot of the source. Drift detection flags an affected mapping. |
| `MEDULLA-UNIFIED-DATA-PLANE-R008.1` | **`ChangeEnvelope`/`ChangeOp` are a typed change-capture record and a validated refusal rule, refusing a change with no durable position or a keyed table's update/delete with no key.** `crates/medulla-durable/src/change_envelope.rs` defines the `Insert`/`Update`/`Delete` op enum and the envelope's `source_position`/`table`/`op`/`key`/`table_is_keyless` fields, and `ChangeEnvelope::validate` refuses an empty `source_position` and refuses an `Update`/`Delete` with no `key` unless `table_is_keyless` is set. CdcHub, durable position storage, replay, lag metrics, the WAL retention cap, and catalog-drift detection are out of scope for this child. Caller chain: no production caller yet. The planned chain is a capture adapter (R015.2.2.2 watermark poll, R021 Debezium bridge) → `ChangeEnvelope::validate` → conversion to a mutation envelope through the approved mapping → `Method::ApplyChangeEnvelopes` → `dispatch_change_envelopes` (`crates/medulla-server-graph/src/dispatch/change_envelope.rs`); an invalid capture record is refused before it reaches the graph writer. Shape: `crates/medulla-durable/src/change_envelope.rs`: `ChangeOp` (`Insert`, `Update`, `Delete`), `ChangeEnvelope { source_position, table, op, key, table_is_keyless }`, `InvalidChangeEnvelope`, `validate` (line 94). This is the capture record, distinct from the wire mutation envelope `crates/medulla-protocol-types/src/change_envelope.rs::ChangeEnvelope`; R015.2.2.2 and R021 reuse this type and convert at the apply step. | Verified by unit tests: a keyed table's insert with no key validates, a keyed table's update with a key validates, an empty `source_position` is refused, a keyed table's update with no key is refused, and an explicitly keyless table's update with no key validates. Existing tests: `crates/medulla-durable/src/change_envelope.rs::insert_with_no_key_on_a_keyed_table_validates`, `crates/medulla-durable/src/change_envelope.rs::update_with_a_key_on_a_keyed_table_validates`, `crates/medulla-durable/src/change_envelope.rs::empty_source_position_is_refused`, `crates/medulla-durable/src/change_envelope.rs::update_with_no_key_on_a_keyed_table_is_refused`, `crates/medulla-durable/src/change_envelope.rs::update_with_no_key_on_an_explicitly_keyless_table_validates`. |
| `MEDULLA-UNIFIED-DATA-PLANE-R007.1` | **`DialectPushdownCapabilities` declares a dialect's supported pushdown operations, refusing an unknown operation name.** `crates/medulla-federation/src/federation_capabilities.rs` parses the five named operations (projection, filter, limit, join, aggregate) and refuses the whole capability declaration if any named operation is not one of them. Caller chain: no production caller yet. The planned chain is a dialect entry (`SqlEngineDialectEntry`, `crates/medulla-types/src/attached_source_dialect.rs`) → `DialectPushdownCapabilities::declare` → the shared renderer of R006 in `crates/medulla-federation/src/federation_opt`, which consults the declared operations before pushing a fragment. Shape: `crates/medulla-federation/src/federation_capabilities.rs`: `PushdownOperation` (projection, filter, limit, join, aggregate) with `parse`, `UnknownPushdownOperation`, and `DialectPushdownCapabilities::declare(&[&str])`, which refuses the whole declaration on one unknown name. Reuse `parse`; add no free-text capability strings. | Verified by unit tests: all five names parse, an unknown name is refused, declaring all valid names succeeds, and one bad name among good ones refuses the whole declaration. Existing tests: `crates/medulla-federation/src/federation_capabilities.rs::all_five_names_parse`, `crates/medulla-federation/src/federation_capabilities.rs::an_unknown_name_is_refused`, `crates/medulla-federation/src/federation_capabilities.rs::declaring_all_valid_names_succeeds`, `crates/medulla-federation/src/federation_capabilities.rs::declaring_one_bad_name_refuses_the_whole_set`. |
| `MEDULLA-UNIFIED-DATA-PLANE-R009` | **Drive consumers from change capture idempotently.** Change events update the graph through approved mappings, refresh vector and full-text indexes, and emit CEP and outbox events, all idempotent on the source position so a replayed event produces no duplicate effect. Net-new (no idempotent-consumer type existed on main): split per the net-new rule. `.1` is the typed checkpoint/idempotent-apply model. Rollup: LANDED only once every child is. | A replay test re-delivers the same change position and confirms each consumer's state is unchanged. |
| `MEDULLA-UNIFIED-DATA-PLANE-R010` | **Maintain an accelerated local copy of attached data.** Medulla maintains an optional cached virtual graph or columnar replica in medulla-lake for attached tables, refreshed by change capture under a per-table acceleration policy. Depends on MEDULLA-UNIFIED-DATA-PLANE-R008 (change capture), which is being split concurrently. Split per the net-new rule (no typed acceleration-policy model existed on main): `.1` is the typed model. Rollup: LANDED only once every child is. | A restart/failover test shows the accelerated copy reconverges to the source within its declared lag objective. |
| `MEDULLA-UNIFIED-DATA-PLANE-R010.1` | **`ReplicaRefreshPolicy` is a typed per-table policy, refusing a declaration its own verification could never check.** `crates/medulla-lake/src/acceleration_policy.rs` renames `AccelerationPolicy` to `ReplicaRefreshPolicy`. The mapping-level `AccelerationPolicy` of R038.1 in `medulla-types` keeps its name. The policy validates that a declared policy names a real table. When `enabled`, it declares a non-zero lag objective. An enabled policy with no lag objective cannot be measured against a reconverge-within-lag test. A disabled policy may carry `lag_objective_ms == 0`. Caller chain: no production caller yet. The planned chain is the acceleration refresh loop (R010 children) → `ReplicaRefreshPolicy::validate` when a per-table policy is declared → refresh from `ConsumerCheckpoint` positions (R009.1) into `medulla-lake`; the reconverge-within-lag test reads `lag_objective_ms`. Shape: `crates/medulla-lake/src/acceleration_policy.rs` today names the struct `AccelerationPolicy` (line 16) and the error `InvalidAccelerationPolicy`; this row renames them to `ReplicaRefreshPolicy` and `InvalidReplicaRefreshPolicy` and updates the in-file tests, so it no longer clashes with `crates/medulla-types/src/metadata_acceleration.rs::AccelerationPolicy` (R038.1). Fields stay `table`, `enabled`, `lag_objective_ms`; `validate` stays as is. Nothing outside the file uses the old name. | Verified by unit tests. An enabled policy with a positive lag objective validates. A disabled policy with `lag_objective_ms == 0` validates. An empty table name is refused regardless of `enabled`. An enabled policy with `lag_objective_ms == 0` is refused. Existing tests: `crates/medulla-lake/src/acceleration_policy.rs::an_enabled_policy_with_a_positive_lag_objective_validates`, `crates/medulla-lake/src/acceleration_policy.rs::a_disabled_policy_with_no_lag_objective_validates`, `crates/medulla-lake/src/acceleration_policy.rs::an_empty_table_name_is_refused_regardless_of_enabled`, `crates/medulla-lake/src/acceleration_policy.rs::an_enabled_policy_with_no_lag_objective_is_refused`. The rename itself is checked by the compiler plus `grep ReplicaRefreshPolicy`. |
| `MEDULLA-UNIFIED-DATA-PLANE-R009.1` | **`ConsumerCheckpoint` applies an incoming position idempotently, refusing an unnamed consumer.** `crates/medulla-durable/src/idempotent_consumer.rs` refuses a checkpoint with no `consumer_id`, and `apply_if_newer` is a no-op on a replayed or stale position rather than reapplying it. Caller chain: no production caller yet. The planned chain is a capture record (`medulla_durable::change_envelope::ChangeEnvelope`) → `dispatch_change_envelopes` (`crates/medulla-server-graph/src/dispatch/change_envelope.rs`) → each consumer (graph apply, vector and full-text refresh, CEP, outbox) calls `ConsumerCheckpoint::apply_if_newer(position)` first and skips its effect when it returns `false`. Shape: `crates/medulla-durable/src/idempotent_consumer.rs`: `ConsumerCheckpoint { consumer_id: String, position: u64 }`, `validate` (refuses an empty or blank id with `EmptyConsumerId`) and `apply_if_newer(&mut self, u64) -> bool` (strictly newer applies; a replay or stale position returns `false`). One checkpoint row per consumer id is stored through the persistence seam of R008; reuse this type, add no second checkpoint. | Verified by unit tests: a named checkpoint validates, an empty `consumer_id` is refused, a strictly newer position applies, and replaying the same position a second time is an idempotent no-op. | `MEDULLA-UNIFIED-DATA-PLANE-R011` | **Route queries by freshness across native, live, and accelerated paths.** The query router selects between the native store, a live source read, and the accelerated copy based on declared freshness policy, and a client requesting read-your-writes waits for its last committed source position up to a bounded timeout rather than returning a stale result. Caller chain: no production caller yet. The planned chain is `handle_unified_query` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/sql_read.rs:309`) → the freshness router (R011 children) → `ReadYourWritesWait::new(timeout_ms)` for a read-your-writes request → `WaitOutcome`; the chosen `FreshnessRoute::label` is printed by EXPLAIN (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/explain_handlers.rs`). Shape: `crates/medulla-plan/src/freshness_router.rs`: `FreshnessRoute` (`Native`, `Live`, `Accelerated`) with `label`, `ReadYourWritesWait { timeout_ms }` with `new` (refuses zero with `UnboundedWait`), `WaitOutcome` (`Satisfied`, `TimedOutTyped`). Reuse these types in the router; add no stale-return path. | Verified by unit tests: a positive timeout constructs, a zero timeout is refused, the two wait outcomes are distinct, and all three routes are known. | `MEDULLA-UNIFIED-DATA-PLANE-R015.2.2.1` | **Lock-free WAL snapshot reader.** Subsystem: sources. Code: `crates/medulla-sqlite-format/src/wal.rs` (new): `WalSnapshot::open(main: &Path, wal: &Path) -> Result<WalSnapshot, SqliteReadRefusal>`. The function parses the 32-byte WAL header (magic `0x377f0682` or `0x377f0683`, page size, two salts). It walks frames, validates salts and the running checksum, and stops at the last frame with a non-zero database-size field. The page map keeps the newest frame per page. `reader.rs` reads pages through that overlay. After the read it re-reads the WAL header salts. A change means a checkpoint raced the read, so the read retries until `foreign.sqlite.busy_timeout_ms` elapses. Then it refuses with `SQLITE_SNAPSHOT_UNSTABLE`. The open flags are read-only (`mode=ro`); the file is never written. Depends on: MEDULLA-UNIFIED-DATA-PLANE-R015.2.1. Caller chain: `handle_unified_query` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/sql_read.rs:309`) → `FederatedExec` → `federation::source_for` (`crates/medulla-federation/src/federation.rs:109`) → new `medulla_plan::sqlite_foreign::fetch` (R015.2.2.3) → `WalSnapshot::open` (new, `crates/medulla-sqlite-format/src/wal.rs`) → `Reader` (`crates/medulla-sqlite-format/src/reader.rs:22`) reading pages through the WAL overlay. Shape: new file `crates/medulla-sqlite-format/src/wal.rs` (declared in `crates/medulla-sqlite-format/src/lib.rs`): `WalSnapshot` holds the page map `BTreeMap<u32, Vec<u8>>`, the header salts and the database size in pages; `SqliteReadRefusal` joins the crate's error type in `crates/medulla-sqlite-format/src/error.rs`. `Reader::from_bytes` (reader.rs:36) and `Reader::open` (reader.rs:30) get a page-overlay constructor so no second page decoder exists. The crate stays free of the engine; the busy timeout is passed in by the caller. | Test: `crates/medulla-sqlite-format/tests/wal_snapshot.rs::snapshot_sees_last_commit_and_survives_checkpoint_race` (L2) builds a WAL with a torn tail frame and a racing checkpoint. |

| `MEDULLA-UNIFIED-DATA-PLANE-R022.2` | **Matrix manifest and runner entry per engine version.** Subsystem: sources, quality. Medulla owns `conformance/attached_matrix.toml` and the target `tests/attached_conformance.rs`. The target reads the manifest. It reads each engine's DSN from the variable `MEDULLA_CONFORMANCE_<ENGINE>_DSN`, which the lane sets. It skips an engine whose variable is unset and prints a visible skip line. It never starts containers; the pipelines workflow does. Entries: `postgres:16`, `mysql:8.4`, `mariadb:11.4`, `clickhouse/clickhouse-server`, plus the `SQLite` fixture. Each pins an image digest before it counts as green. The release workflow that builds each engine image outputs the digest and pins it in its release manifest; the `digest` of an entry is copied unchanged from that manifest and is never resolved at run time or typed by hand. Depends on: MEDULLA-UNIFIED-DATA-PLANE-R022. Caller chain: the nightly workflow of `PIPE-GATES-R020` → `cargo test --test attached_conformance` → `tests/attached_conformance.rs::manifest_pins_every_image_digest`, which reads `conformance/attached_matrix.toml` and, for each engine, `MEDULLA_CONFORMANCE_<ENGINE>_DSN`. Shape: new files `conformance/attached_matrix.toml` (`[[engine]]` tables with `dialect`, `image`, `digest` (`sha256:` and 64 lowercase hex, the value in the release manifest entry for the same image), `version`) and `tests/attached_conformance.rs` registered as a `[[test]]` in the root `Cargo.toml`; the test parses the manifest with the `toml` crate already in the workspace and builds each result through `DialectConformanceEntry` (R022.1). An unset DSN variable prints `skip: <engine>` and counts as not green. | Test: `tests/attached_conformance.rs::manifest_pins_every_image_digest` (L1) asserts every entry's digest has the `sha256:` form and no entry is unpinned. The nightly run is the L3 proof. |

P1: run `conformance/fixtures/gramps_corpus.sql` on the `postgres:16` container and record each statement's result digest and SQLSTATE. P2: replay the same corpus unmodified on the engine and record each failure with its SQLSTATE. The P2 artifact lists every statement that differs from P1. This replaces the traffic corpus of retired R028.3. Refusals: a phase with an invalid exit stops the gate with `PHASE_EXIT_INVALID`. Writes: artifact files only. Depends on: MEDULLA-UNIFIED-DATA-PLANE-R026.2.1 and the statement corpus of connector spec `CONN-GRAMPS-MCP-CONNECTOR-CONTENT`. Caller chain: `tests/native_hosting_gramps.rs::p0_p1_p2_exit_artifacts` → the pgwire listener (`crates/medulla-wire/src/pgwire/mod.rs`) as an external client → `GrampsBaselineDigest::to_phase_exit` (`crates/medulla-types/src/gramps_baseline_digest.rs:43`) → artifact file `conformance/native_hosting/<phase>.json`. Shape: new root test target `tests/native_hosting_gramps.rs` (registered as `[[test]]` in the root `Cargo.toml`) and fixture `conformance/fixtures/gramps_corpus.sql`; the test writes artifacts with `serde_json`, builds P0 through `to_phase_exit`, runs the corpus against `postgres:16` (DSN from `MEDULLA_CONFORMANCE_POSTGRES_DSN`, as R022.2) and against the engine, and lists differing statements by SQLSTATE and result digest. `PHASE_EXIT_INVALID` is a constant in the test support module. The test is a client only; it links no handler crate. | Test: `tests/native_hosting_gramps.rs::p0_p1_p2_exit_artifacts` (L3) asserts a valid exit for P0, P1 and P2 and a stored difference list for P2. |

| `MEDULLA-UNIFIED-DATA-PLANE-R031.2` | **Per-source quota metering and fault isolation.** Subsystem: sources. The source record carries `quota { max_concurrent, max_rows, max_wall_ms }`. Defaults come from server config `foreign.default_quota`. `max_concurrent` is 8 with bounds [1, 256]. `max_rows` is 250000 with bounds [1, 10000000]. `max_wall_ms` is 60000 with bounds [100, 600000]. Caller chain: `handle_unified_query` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/sql_read.rs:309`) → `FederatedExec` (`crates/medulla-federation/src/federation_opt/federated.rs`) → `foreign_scan` (`crates/medulla-federation/src/federation_opt/run.rs:26`) → per-source limiter (`crates/medulla-federation/src/federation_opt/limiter.rs`) and `FederationBudget` (`crates/medulla-federation/src/federation_opt/budget.rs`). | `MEDULLA-UNIFIED-DATA-PLANE-R037` | **Virtual-graph vocabulary is a Medulla core schema source.** Medulla ships `core:virtual-graph@1` with three classes: `SourceConnection`, `MetadataContract` and `VirtualMapping`. A `SourceConnection` carries a source kind, an endpoint reference and declared capabilities. Source kinds cover API, MCP, A2A, GraphQL, SQL, Iceberg, Teradata-style warehouses and SPARQL endpoints. A `MetadataContract` describes one source at one schema version under a content digest. It lists discovered entities, fields, relationships and operations. A `VirtualMapping` binds one ontology class to one discovered entity, with a key field and predicate-to-field pairs. SHACL shapes refuse an endpoint reference with credentials. They also refuse a mapping that names an undiscovered entity or field. Only an approved mapping serves reads. Caller chain: Arbora `manage` graph-schema attach → `GraphSchema` admin op (`crates/medulla-h-admin/src/graph_schema`) → `GraphSchemaSources` (`crates/medulla-graph-core/src/graph/schema_sources.rs`, core vocabulary and shapes loaded at lines 1121 and 1144) → `validate_and_compose` (`crates/medulla-server-api/src/schema_compose.rs:53`) on every graph that composes the schema. Shape: the vocabulary is `crates/medulla-core/ontology/virtual_graph-v1.ttl` (classes `vg:SourceConnection`, `vg:MetadataContract`, `vg:VirtualMapping`) and the SHACL shapes are `crates/medulla-core/ontology/virtual_graph-v1.shapes.ttl`, both included as core sources in `schema_sources.rs`; pack or ingestion attaches that claim an approved mapping are refused (`document_claims_virtual_graph_approval`, schema_sources.rs:430). Reuse these files; the typed approval state of R005.1 is the runtime twin. | Shape fixtures accept a valid connection, contract and approved mapping. They reject credential-bearing references, undiscovered fields and unapproved mappings at read time. Existing tests: `crates/medulla-h-admin/src/graph_schema/virtual_graph_tests.rs::the_virtual_graph_vocabulary_and_shapes_are_core_sources`, `crates/medulla-h-admin/src/graph_schema/virtual_graph_tests.rs::a_well_formed_connection_contract_and_approved_mapping_conforms`, `crates/medulla-h-admin/src/graph_schema/virtual_graph_tests.rs::each_planted_defect_is_flagged`. |
| `MEDULLA-UNIFIED-DATA-PLANE-R038` | **Metadata is materialized, entity rows stay at the source, and only a watermark-bound cache holds copies (rollup).** Subsystem: Sources. Entry: served `UnifiedQuery`, SPARQL and openCypher reads. Caller: read handler → `foreign_scan` at `crates/medulla-federation/src/federation_opt/run.rs:26` → live or copy route. Discovery writes a `MetadataContract` only (`MEDULLA-UNIFIED-DATA-PLANE-R038.1`) and never copies entity rows. Entity rows answer live through approved virtual mappings. A copy is an expiring cache, never a second store. A copy needs the source `copy` capability, a hot-subset predicate and a live watermark. Knobs: `knob acceleration.max_copy_rows` default 1000000, range [1000, 100000000], unit rows; `knob acceleration.watermark_ttl_ms` default 300000, range [1000, 86400000], unit ms. | `MEDULLA-UNIFIED-DATA-PLANE-R038.1` | **`MetadataContract`/`AccelerationPolicy` are typed models with a capability refusal and a watermark-missing live fallback.** `crates/medulla-types/src/metadata_acceleration.rs` defines `MetadataContract` and `AccelerationPolicy`. `MetadataContract` is source-kind-generic, metadata only, with no row data field. `validate_policy` refuses a policy that declares a hot-subset predicate for a source with no `copy` capability. `AccelerationPolicy::resolve_mode` returns `FragmentExecutionMode::Live` whenever the copy capability, predicate or freshness watermark is missing. It returns `Copy` only when all three are present. `plan_evolution` is a consumer of `validate_policy` for `materialize_subgraph`. Caller chain: `handle_unified_query` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/sql_read.rs:309`) → `FederatedExec` (`crates/medulla-federation/src/federation_opt/federated.rs`) → `foreign_scan` (`crates/medulla-federation/src/federation_opt/run.rs:26`) → route choice by `AccelerationPolicy::resolve_mode` (`crates/medulla-types/src/metadata_acceleration.rs:140`); `validate_policy` (line 178) runs at policy declaration and in `plan_evolution` for `materialize_subgraph`. Shape: `crates/medulla-types/src/metadata_acceleration.rs`: `MetadataContract { source_kind, source_name, schema_version, content_hash }` (no row-data field), `AccelerationPolicy`, `FragmentExecutionMode` (`Live`, `Copy`), `validate_policy` and `InvalidMetadataContract`. `resolve_mode` returns `Copy` only with the copy capability, a hot-subset predicate and a fresh watermark. This `AccelerationPolicy` is the mapping-level policy; the table-level one in `medulla-lake` is `ReplicaRefreshPolicy` (R010.1). | Verified by unit tests. A well-formed contract validates. An empty source kind is refused. A fully declared policy resolves to `Copy`. A missing watermark falls back to `Live`. A mapping with no predicate is `Live`. A predicate declared without copy capability is refused. Existing tests: `crates/medulla-types/src/metadata_acceleration.rs::well_formed_contract_validates`, `crates/medulla-types/src/metadata_acceleration.rs::contract_with_empty_source_kind_is_refused`, `crates/medulla-types/src/metadata_acceleration.rs::fully_declared_policy_resolves_to_copy`, `crates/medulla-types/src/metadata_acceleration.rs::missing_watermark_falls_back_to_live`, `crates/medulla-types/src/metadata_acceleration.rs::cold_mapping_with_no_predicate_is_live`, `crates/medulla-types/src/metadata_acceleration.rs::predicate_without_copy_capability_is_refused`. |

The session keeps `HashMap<Fingerprint,SnapshotHandle>`. `SqlRemote::run` reuses the held connection when a handle exists. The handle drops at query end and rolls back. Rule: the first fragment of a source runs `BEGIN ISOLATION LEVEL REPEATABLE READ READ ONLY`, then `pg_export_snapshot()`. Later fragments import the token with `SET TRANSACTION SNAPSHOT`. A declared replica serves only when its replay lag is at most `knob federation.replica_lag_bound_ms`. Each statement stops at `knob federation.statement_timeout_ms`. Refusals: see `.4`. | `MEDULLA-UNIFIED-DATA-PLANE-R040.3` | **Wiring: the gate runs in the activation path before a binding with a new model version goes `Live`.** Subsystem: Query and reasoning. Entry: the S6 activation transition, `finalize_generation`. `TransitionBinding` accepts only `Building` and `Disabled` and never makes a binding `Live`, so the gate is not there. Caller chain: `finalize_generation` (`crates/medulla-ann-codes/src/semantic_ann_codes/activation.rs:58`, the only route to `Live`, reached from the S6 stage of the worker) → `live_transition` (`crates/medulla-ann-codes/src/semantic_ann_codes/activation.rs:303`) → `promotion_gate` (new, `crates/medulla-ann-codes/src/semantic_ann_codes/promotion.rs`). Shape: `promotion_gate(binding, generation)->Result<PromotionVerdict,PromotionBlocked>` in the new `crates/medulla-ann-codes/src/semantic_ann_codes/promotion.rs`. It loads the stored `ModalityCheckRecord` list for the binding's model: `ModalityCheckRecord { model_id, model_version, binding_id, check: ModalityCheck, recorded_at_ms }`, a typed catalog record (new, `crates/medulla-types/src/embedding_promotion.rs`) written through the catalog write path, one per model, binding and modality. It reads measured recall at k per modality from `StageReceipt { binding_id, stage, modality, recall: f64, measured_at_ms }`, a typed catalog record the S4 and S5 consumers write when a stage completes (`recall` in [0, 1]). It then calls `evaluate_promotion` (`crates/medulla-types/src/embedding_promotion.rs:122`). On a pass it calls `EmbeddingVersionStore::tag_batch` (`crates/medulla-ann/src/version.rs:129`) for the binding's vector ids; this is the first production caller of `tag_batch`. `EmbeddingVersionStore` is persisted per binding in the vector index store, with a listing of the binding's vector ids, so `tag_batch` reads and writes durable state. Rule: the gate applies when the generation's model id or version differs from the binding's current `Live` generation. The binding validation at `crates/medulla-table-store/src/tables/embedding_binding.rs:526` (`validate_binding`) runs first. Refusals: see `.4`. Evidence: see `.5`. Write path: the activation and the verdict record commit in the one semantic-store commit of `finalize_generation`, so they are atomic; no `MutationBatch` is involved. Depends on `MEDULLA-UNIFIED-DATA-PLANE-R040.1`, `MEDULLA-UNIFIED-DATA-PLANE-R040.2`. The dependency loop with `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R077.3` is broken one way: R077.3 depends on this row and this row never depends on R077.3. | Test: `crates/medulla-ann/src/version.rs::tests::tag_requires_passing_verdict` (L2) and `crates/medulla-ann-codes/src/semantic_ann_codes/promotion.rs::tests::activation_runs_gate` (L3). |
| `MEDULLA-UNIFIED-DATA-PLANE-R040.4` | **Refusal: a blocked promotion leaves the prior version active.** Subsystem: Query and reasoning. Entry: the S6 activation transition. Rule: a failed verdict refuses the activation with `PROMOTION_BLOCKED`. The detail lists each regressed modality with its baseline and measured value. The prior generation stays `Live` and the active pointer does not move. The gate calls neither `tag` nor `tag_batch`. A modality without a probe set or measurement also blocks (`evaluate_promotion` already treats it as failed). Evidence: the verdict record of `.5` is still written, in its own semantic-store commit because the activation commit does not happen. Write path: verdict record only. Depends on `MEDULLA-UNIFIED-DATA-PLANE-R040.3`. Shape: `PROMOTION_BLOCKED` is a registered `ServerErrorCode` variant (`crates/medulla-types/src/contract/server_error_code.rs`, routed for `SemanticIndex` in `crates/medulla-capabilities/src/error_routing.rs`) and a `pub const` beside `promotion_gate` in `crates/medulla-ann-codes/src/semantic_ann_codes/promotion.rs`; `finalize_generation` returns it as a `SemanticCodeError::Refused` that the S6 stage reports to the binding status read by `SemanticIndexOp::StageStatus`. | Test: `crates/medulla-ann/src/version.rs::tests::blocked_promotion_tags_nothing` (L2) asserts no vector carries the candidate version after a block. |
| `MEDULLA-UNIFIED-DATA-PLANE-R040.5` | **Evidence: each `PromotionVerdict` is a typed record linked to the model, version and binding.** Subsystem: Query and reasoning. Entry: the S6 activation transition. Caller chain: `finalize_generation` (`crates/medulla-ann-codes/src/semantic_ann_codes/activation.rs:58`, the only route to `Live`, reached from the S6 stage of the worker) → `live_transition` (`crates/medulla-ann-codes/src/semantic_ann_codes/activation.rs:303`) → `promotion_gate` (new, `crates/medulla-ann-codes/src/semantic_ann_codes/promotion.rs`) → the semantic-store commit. Shape: `PromotionVerdict` (`crates/medulla-types/src/embedding_promotion.rs:68`) is stored as a typed semantic-store record keyed by binding id and generation, naming the model id and version, with one `ModalityVerdict` entry per modality (metric, floor, baseline, measured, probe set digest and `passed`). Digests are SHA-256, 64 lowercase hex characters. The record is read back by `SemanticIndexOp::StageStatus` and by UQL over the catalog. Write path: the same semantic-store commit as the activation on a pass, so the two are atomic. Refusals: none. Depends on `MEDULLA-UNIFIED-DATA-PLANE-R040.3`. | Test: `crates/medulla-types/src/embedding_promotion.rs::tests::verdict_is_stored_with_modality_entries` (L1) and `tests/embedding/test_promotion_gate_served.py::test_verdict_readable_by_query` (L4). |
| `MEDULLA-UNIFIED-DATA-PLANE-R040.6` | **Hot-path proof: a regressed image check blocks a served promotion.** Subsystem: Query and reasoning. Entry: a served row write on a bound text column whose re-embedding reaches the S6 activation. Fixture: a candidate model whose recorded image recall is below its floor (`tests/embedding/fixtures/regressed_image.json`, new). The test creates `tests/embedding/` (new). Rule: the activation refuses with `PROMOTION_BLOCKED` (read from the binding status), the prior version stays `Live`, and a verdict record names the image modality. Nightly bench: none. Refusals: `PROMOTION_BLOCKED`. Write path: verdict record. Depends on `MEDULLA-UNIFIED-DATA-PLANE-R040.3`, `MEDULLA-UNIFIED-DATA-PLANE-R040.5`. Caller chain: served `Sql` write → `queue_reembed` → the S6 stage → `finalize_generation` (`crates/medulla-ann-codes/src/semantic_ann_codes/activation.rs:58`, the only route to `Live`, reached from the S6 stage of the worker) → `live_transition` (`crates/medulla-ann-codes/src/semantic_ann_codes/activation.rs:303`) → `promotion_gate` (new, `crates/medulla-ann-codes/src/semantic_ann_codes/promotion.rs`). Shape: the fixture `tests/embedding/fixtures/regressed_image.json` and `tests/embedding/test_promotion_gate_served.py` are new test-only files using the `start_medulla_server` fixture of `tests/conftest.py`. No production file changes. | Test: `tests/embedding/test_promotion_gate_served.py::test_regressed_modality_blocks_promotion` (L4). |

| `MEDULLA-UNIFIED-DATA-PLANE-R026.4` | **Graph-backed relational projections, read-only (rollup).** Subsystem: sources, query. Entry: the `Postgres` wire listener and new `Method::ProjectionRead`. Caller chain: `serve_prepared` (`crates/medulla-wire/src/pgwire/mod.rs:1940`) → `WireSession::execute` (`crates/medulla-wire-core/src/wire/mod.rs:5334`) → `classify` (`crates/medulla-sql-lang/src/sql/classify.rs:520`) → new `Method::ProjectionRead` through the `DispatchApi` re-entry seam (`crates/medulla-server-router/src/dispatch/seam.rs:20`, the seam `try_execute_crossmodal` at `wire/mod.rs:4950` is the model) → `dispatch_graph_op` (`crates/medulla-server-graph/src/dispatch/graph_pipeline.rs:68`) → `medulla_h_query::query` gateway (`crates/medulla-server-graph/src/dispatch/graph_pipeline/gateway.rs:122`) → `dispatch_query_family` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/dispatch.rs:66`) → `projection_read` (new `crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/projection.rs`) → the approved mapping of R005 → the OBDA rewrite `run_outcome_virtual` (`crates/medulla-virtual-graph/src/obda.rs:732`). `medulla-h-query` calls the shared `medulla-virtual-graph` crate, never the sibling `medulla-h-sparql`. The projection exposes graph data as relational tables through an approved, version-pinned mapping. It is read-only. Children `.1` to `.3`. The application's table and column contract and its statement corpus belong to its connector spec `CONN-GRAMPS-MCP-CONNECTOR-CONTENT`. Medulla keeps only the generic mapping (R003, R005, R029). Related: MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R035, MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R043, MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R080. Source SHA-256: `c55bc629b1bfefc9d0346b75b8bb95e5a231b891e7e34297df17b960d6c9773c`. Shape: no code of its own; the children fill `crates/medulla-types/src/projection_read.rs` (new), `crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/projection.rs` (new) and a refusal helper in `crates/medulla-wire-core/src/wire/`. The test file `tests/projection_read_served.rs` is new and follows `tests/served_query_completeness.rs`. | Root L3 test `tests/projection_read_served.rs::projected_table_read_binds_revisions_and_refuses_writes` (L3). |
| `MEDULLA-UNIFIED-DATA-PLANE-R026.4.1` | **Projection read binds mapping and graph revisions.** Subsystem: sources, query. Shape: `ProjectionReadRequest { tenant, actor, graph, mapping_id, mapping_revision, table, columns, predicates, query_digest }` and `ProjectionReadResponse { mapping_revision, graph_revision, column_types, rows, result_digest }` in `crates/medulla-types/src/projection_read.rs` (new). The pgwire session names the mapping in the startup parameter `medulla.mapping` as `id@revision`. Rule: the handler resolves the mapping by id and revision. It reads rows through the OBDA rewrite with the tenant filter. The result digest is SHA-256 over canonical JSON rows sorted by primary key (64 lowercase hex). Refusals: `PROJECTION_MAPPING_UNAPPROVED`, `PROJECTION_MAPPING_STALE`, `PROJECTION_ACCESS_DENIED`. Writes: none. Depends on: MEDULLA-UNIFIED-DATA-PLANE-R005.1, MEDULLA-UNIFIED-DATA-PLANE-R029. Caller chain: `serve_prepared` (`crates/medulla-wire/src/pgwire/mod.rs:1940`) → `WireSession::execute` (`crates/medulla-wire-core/src/wire/mod.rs:5334`) → `classify` (`crates/medulla-sql-lang/src/sql/classify.rs:520`) (the startup parameter `medulla.mapping` is read into `WireSession`, `wire/mod.rs:1226`) → new `Method::ProjectionRead` → `dispatch_graph_op` (`crates/medulla-server-graph/src/dispatch/graph_pipeline.rs:68`) → `medulla_h_query::query` gateway (`crates/medulla-server-graph/src/dispatch/graph_pipeline/gateway.rs:122`) → `dispatch_query_family` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/dispatch.rs:66`) → `projection_read` (new `crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/projection.rs`). | Test: `tests/projection_read_served.rs::projected_read_binds_revisions` (L3) reads a mapped table, asserts both revisions and the digest, and asserts each refusal code. |
| `MEDULLA-UNIFIED-DATA-PLANE-R026.4.2` | **Projected tables refuse every SQL write.** Subsystem: sources, query. Caller chain: `serve_prepared` (`crates/medulla-wire/src/pgwire/mod.rs:1940`) → `WireSession::execute` (`crates/medulla-wire-core/src/wire/mod.rs:5334`) → `classify` (`crates/medulla-sql-lang/src/sql/classify.rs:520`) → the statement kind check in `execute` (`wire/mod.rs:5334`) → `refuse_projection_write` (new, pure, `crates/medulla-wire-core/src/wire/projection.rs`; the wire crate sits below the handlers, so the refusal helper lives beside the classifier and `projection_read` does not call it). An INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE or DDL statement on a projected table refuses with SQLSTATE `25006` and engine code `PROJECTION_READ_ONLY`. The rule applies equally to read-only principals and to principals that hold a separate graph mutation grant. A mutation grant never makes a projection a writer. Writes: none; the graph revision stays unchanged. Depends on: MEDULLA-UNIFIED-DATA-PLANE-R026.4.1. Shape: `refuse_projection_write(kind:&StatementKind, bound_mapping:Option<&str>)->Option<WireError>` returns SQLSTATE `25006` with `PROJECTION_READ_ONLY` for any non-read statement kind when the session names `medulla.mapping`; it never inspects grants. | Test: `tests/projection_read_served.rs::projected_writes_refuse_for_both_principals` (L3) tries four statement kinds as two principals and asserts an unchanged graph revision. |
| `MEDULLA-UNIFIED-DATA-PLANE-R026.4.3` | **Graph changes reach the projection only through MutationBatch.** Subsystem: sources, query. A caller changes projected data by submitting a graph `MutationBatch`. The submission passes the mutation gateway (`crates/medulla-server-graph/src/dispatch/graph_pipeline/gateway.rs`) with the existing policy and admission. A later projection read reflects the committed graph revision. An unauthorized mutation refuses with the gateway's `ACCESS_DENIED`. Writes: only the gateway commits. Depends on: MEDULLA-UNIFIED-DATA-PLANE-R026.4.1, MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R035. Caller chain: a served mutation `Method` → `dispatch_graph_op` (`crates/medulla-server-graph/src/dispatch/graph_pipeline.rs:68`) → `commit_query_gateway` (`crates/medulla-server-graph/src/dispatch/graph_pipeline/gateway.rs:118` region) → `commit_conditional_mutation_async` (`crates/medulla-server-mutation/src/mutation.rs`), then a later `Method::ProjectionRead` (`.1`) reads the new revision. Shape: no production change; the test `tests/projection_read_served.rs::authorized_mutation_is_visible_in_next_read` commits through the existing gateway and reads through `projection_read`. | Test: `tests/projection_read_served.rs::authorized_mutation_is_visible_in_next_read` (L3) commits a batch and asserts the new revision in the next read, plus the unauthorized refusal. |

New `crates/medulla-types/src/document_ops.rs` with `DocumentDeleteRequest{document_id}` and `DocumentDeleteReceipt{plan_digest,nodes_deleted,edges_deleted,index_pending,orphan_report:OrphanReport}`. | `MEDULLA-UNIFIED-DATA-PLANE-R710` | **Message-stream records normalize into a `ChangeEnvelope`: model and behavior.** Subsystem: Ingest. Entry: served `SourceIngest` carrying stream pages. Extends `MEDULLA-UNIFIED-DATA-PLANE-R008`. Decision: the engine consumes no broker. A connector reads the broker (`kafka-mcp` `consume_records`) and submits pages; the engine maps and commits them (`SCION-SOURCE-INGEST-R005.41`). Shape: new `crates/medulla-durable/src/stream_adapter.rs` with `StreamMessage{stream,partition,offset,key,payload,headers,timestamp_ms}` and `fn map_stream_message(msg,&StreamMapping)->Result<ChangeEnvelope,StreamRefusal>`. `ChangeEnvelope` is the medulla-durable type (`crates/medulla-durable/src/change_envelope.rs:47`). Rule: `table` is the stream name. `source_position` is the opaque cursor `<partition>:<offset>`. `op` is `Insert`. `key` is the message key, and a message without a key sets `table_is_keyless`. The engine accepts a page only when each partition's offsets rise by 1 or more. Knobs: `knob stream.max_message_bytes` default 1048576, range [1024, 16777216], unit bytes; `knob stream.page_messages` default 500, range [1, 10000], unit messages. | `MEDULLA-UNIFIED-DATA-PLANE-R038.2.2` | **The copy route is bounded by rows and by watermark.** Subsystem: Sources. Entry: served read. | `MEDULLA-UNIFIED-DATA-PLANE-R763.2` | **Derive column edges from SQL with the shared parser.** Subsystem: Sources. Caller chain: served `Method::ColumnLineage` → `dispatch_graph_op` (`crates/medulla-server-graph/src/dispatch/graph_pipeline.rs:68`) → `medulla_h_query::query` gateway (`crates/medulla-server-graph/src/dispatch/graph_pipeline/gateway.rs:122`) → `dispatch_query_family` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/dispatch.rs:66`) → `handle` (new `crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/column_lineage.rs`) → `medulla_federation::column_lineage::derive_column_lineage` (new). Shape: new `crates/medulla-federation/src/sql_analysis.rs::parse_one_statement(sql, dialect) -> Result<Statement, LineageError>` and `crates/medulla-federation/src/column_lineage.rs::derive_column_lineage(sql, dialect, catalog: &AttachedCatalogGraph, target: &str) -> LineageResult`. Dialect is `medulla_exec::sql_text::SqlDialect` (`crates/medulla-exec/src/lib.rs:94`; Postgres, MySql). Both files are behind feature `federation-sql`. Rule: resolve each output column through FROM aliases, joins, CTEs and subqueries to catalog columns. Star and `t.*` expand from the catalog. UNION maps columns by position. Edge transform: a bare column is `identity`. An aggregate function is `aggregate`. The aggregate functions are `count`, `sum`, `avg`, `min`, `max`, `array_agg` and `string_agg`. Any other expression is `expression`. A column used only in WHERE, ON, GROUP BY or HAVING gives a `filter_only` edge to target column `<target>.*`. Unresolved or ambiguous columns become `LineageGap { output, reason }` in the result. They are never edges. Knob: `knob lineage.max_sql_bytes` default 1048576, [1024, 16777216], bytes. Writes: none; pure function. Depends on: R763.1, R003.1. Also: both new files are declared in `crates/medulla-federation/src/lib.rs` behind `federation-sql`, which already owns the `sqlparser` dependency; `AttachedCatalogGraph` is `crates/medulla-catalog-types/src/attached_catalog.rs:31`. | Test: `crates/medulla-federation/src/column_lineage.rs::tests::view_columns_resolve` (L2, `--features federation-sql`) parses a join view with an aggregate and a star. It asserts every transform kind, a typed gap for an unknown column, and an ambiguous-column gap. Verified by the root slice test `tests/data_plane/test_column_lineage_served.py::test_view_lineage_and_impact` (L3). |

Shape: `handle(ctx, op)->Response` in the new `crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/column_lineage.rs` matches `ColumnLineageOp::{DeriveFromCatalog, DeriveFromSql, Impact}`; the variant sits in `crates/medulla-protocol-types/src/protocol/method/method_10.rs` and the policy in `crates/medulla-capabilities/src/domains/query.rs`. | Test: `tests/data_plane/test_column_lineage_served.py::test_view_lineage_and_impact` (L3) attaches a source with a view and asks the impact of one column. Shape: add `QuarantineReason::SqlUnparseable(String)` and `SqlTooLarge` to `crates/medulla-catalog-types/src/lineage.rs`; codes `LINEAGE_SQL_UNPARSEABLE` and `LINEAGE_SQL_TOO_LARGE`. Rule: a parse error or an oversized statement writes one `QuarantinedLineageEvent`. It holds the SHA-256 digest of the SQL text, 64 lowercase hex characters. The call returns the record; it writes no edge for that statement. Other statements of the same call still derive. Registered in the family `REFUSALS` table (R010.100). Writes: the quarantine record through the R702.4 quarantine store. Depends on: R763.3, R702.4. | Test: `tests/data_plane/test_column_lineage_served.py::test_unparseable_sql_quarantines` (L3) sends one bad and one good view. Writes: none. Depends on: MEDULLA-TYPED-PACKS-R108.16. Shape: the three codes are `CatalogRefusal::code()` strings in `crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/catalog_search.rs` and are registered in the family `REFUSALS` table. | Test: `tests/data_plane/test_catalog_search_served.py::test_search_refusals` (L3) asserts the three codes. A caller without access to an asset gets no hit and an unchanged total. |

| `MEDULLA-UNIFIED-DATA-PLANE-R767` | **Tenant context view over approved sources.** Subsystem: unified data plane. Caller chain: served `Method::GetTenantContext` → `dispatch_graph_op` (`crates/medulla-server-graph/src/dispatch/graph_pipeline.rs:68`) → `medulla_h_query::query` gateway (`crates/medulla-server-graph/src/dispatch/graph_pipeline/gateway.rs:122`) → `dispatch_query_family` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/dispatch.rs:66`) → `handle_tenant_context` (new `crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/tenant_context.rs`) → `ForeignSourceCatalog::registry_for` (`crates/medulla-server-ops/src/api/query_txn/foreign_catalog.rs:178`); `UnifiedQuery` with `context_view` → `handle_unified_query` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/query/sql_read.rs:309`) → `FederatedExec::new` (`planning.rs:408`). The method returns the approved source bindings of the caller: `{source_id, category, mapping_id, clearance}` for organizations, roles, data assets, permissions and time-series facts. `UnifiedQuery` accepts a `context_view` id and runs through the logical view without copying source data.
